MSCI 降評風險升高，印尼股市保守看待
2026 年 10 月 27 日
一、 市場動態
1. MSCI 擬將印尼降至獨立市場，降評風險明顯升高：MSCI 於 10 月 15 日宣布，啟動
將印尼股市由「新興市場（Emerging Market）」直接降至「獨立市場（Standalone
Market） 」的諮詢程序，意見徵詢將於2027 年 4 月 30 日截止，最晚於 2027 年 6 月
30 日公布最終決定 。MSCI 原先於今年 1 月提出可能降至 「邊境市場 （Frontier Market
） 」 ，此次則跳過「邊境市場」提出直接將該國股市降至「獨立市場」的方案，顯示對

印尼市場治理的疑慮進一步加深。雖然「邊境市場」仍可能是替代分類方案，但MSCI
強調，除非有明確證據顯示市場治理及運作出現實質改善，否則預期將依目前提案重
新分類，顯示此次降評風險較過去顯著升高。
2. 股權透明度與交易秩序問題為 MSCI 主要疑慮：MSCI 指出，印尼股市長期存在股權
結構不透明、實際自由流通股比例難以確認、疑似協調交易，以及市場監理執法成效
不足等問題，影響國際投資人對市場公平性與可投資性的信心。雖然印尼主管機關已
提出強化持股超過 1%股東資訊揭露、建立高持股集中度（HSC）監理機制，以及規
劃逐步將最低自由流通股比例提高至 15%等改革措施，但MSCI 認為相關改革尚未展
現持續且具實質性的改善成果。由於問題涉及市場制度與治理結構，改善所需時間可
能較一般短期市場波動更長。
3. 股市反彈後再度回落，外資撤離壓力仍高：受降評消息衝擊，印尼雅加達綜合指數 10
月 16 日重挫 11.57%，創 1998 年以來最大單日跌幅，19 日再跌 3.64%，盤中最低
觸及 4,907.37 點 。 雖然20 日至 23 日連續四個交易日反彈 ， 但26 日再度下跌 1.22%

，收於 5,373.09 點，各產業類股全面收黑，顯示市場信心仍不穩定。根據 Reuters
報導，截至 10 月 19 日，2026 年外資已累計淨賣超印尼股票約 48.8 億美元。此外，
印尼證交所 26 日宣布，將引進獨立專家及外國投資人代表參與高持股集中度審查，
並自 11 月起加強調查疑似協調交易，必要時凍結可疑帳戶，以回應 MSCI 對市場制
度的疑慮。
二、 下跌原因分析：
1. 股市：潛在指數剔除與資金外流，持續壓抑市場評價
若印尼股市最終降至「獨立市場」， 原則上將不再納入MSCI 新興市場及邊境市場相關
指數，追蹤相關指數的被動式基金將須依規則調整持股，實際處理方式仍以 MSCI 後
續公告為準。若改降至「邊境市場」，則可能獲得部分邊境市場資金承接，衝擊相對較
小。Reuters 引述多家金融機構估計，印尼股市降至獨立市場可能引發約 30 億至 60

億美元被動資金流出，其中 UBS 估計約 40 億美元，並指出主動型基金另有約 150 億
美元潛在撤資風險，惟實際規模仍視最終分類結果及基金策略而定。
2. 匯率：外資撤離增加印尼盾貶值壓力
印尼股市的降評風險不僅衝擊股市，也可能透過外資撤離增加印尼盾貶值壓力。印尼
盾 10 月 16 日盤中一度貶至 18,070 兌 1 美元，為 7 月下旬以來最弱水準之一，當日
收盤約 18,000。若後續國際資金持續流出，可能進一步增加股匯市波動，對以外幣計
價、且未完全避險的印尼基金及 ETF 形成額外壓力。雖然印尼央行可透過匯市干預及

流動性管理措施穩定金融市場，但在資金外流壓力尚未緩解前，印尼盾仍可能維持偏
弱格局。
3. 基金與 ETF：指數權重下滑，單一市場曝險風險升高
MSCI 相關指數調整已對印尼股票產生實質影響。Reuters 引用 LSEG 資料顯示，印尼
在 MSCI 新興市場指數的權重已由 2026 年初的 1.16%降至 9 月底的 0.43%，相關成
分股亦由年初 18 檔減至 9 檔，反映部分指數資金調整壓力已提前出現。對印尼單一
國家基金及 ETF 而言，主要風險來自股市下跌、資金流出及印尼盾波動；主動式基金
雖可透過選股降低部分影響，但仍難以完全迴避整體市場風險。不同產品實際受到的
影響，仍應依追蹤指數、主要持股及匯率避險安排個別評估。
三、 市場看法：
1. 制度性問題難以速解，降評風險將持續牽動市場：我們認為，此次 MSCI 降評風險並
非單純反映經濟成長放緩或企業獲利不佳，而是涉及市場透明度、交易公平性與監理
執法等結構性問題。尤其 MSCI 不僅啟動降評諮詢，更持續限制印尼股票的部分指數

調整，包括不提高外資納入因子、不新增相關成分股，以及不進行向上規模分類調整
，顯示市場可投資性疑慮尚未獲得有效解決。儘管政府試圖推動改革，但制度修正仍
須經過執行與成效驗證，才能重新取得國際投資人的信任，因此短期內降評風險仍難
完全消除。
2. 外資撤離與指數權重下降，市場信心修復恐需較長時間：印尼今年以來外資已大幅賣
超，MSCI 新興市場指數中的權重亦明顯下降，顯示國際投資人對當地市場的疑慮已
逐漸反映於實際資金流向。我們認為，即使印尼最終成功避免降評，部分國際機構投
資人仍可能因市場透明度、流動性及治理品質疑慮而維持較低曝險。此外，MSCI 對
部分指數調整的凍結措施尚未解除，短期內新增指數資金流入機會仍受到限制，預期
國際資金回流與市場評價修復均可能需要較長時間。
3. 監理改革力度加大，但尚不足以扭轉保守展望：印尼政府近期持續加強改革，包括提
高資訊透明度、強化交易監控、對違規交易開罰，以及引進外部獨立人士參與監理審
查。10 月 26 日該國政府進一步提出主動調查疑似協調交易及凍結可疑帳戶等措施，

11 月 2 日起亦規劃將高持股集中度股票排除於雅加達綜合指數之外 。 相關改革有助改

善交易秩序及短期市場信心，但部分措施仍待正式實施，且實際成效尚未獲得 MSCI
認可。考量制度性問題改善需要時間，政策利多雖可能帶動短線反彈，但尚不足以改
變對印尼股市中期前景的保守判斷。
4. 股市跌深不代表利空出盡，中期風險報酬吸引力偏低：印尼股市在 10 月 16 日及 19
日大幅修正後，雖曾連續四個交易日反彈，但 26 日再度回落，顯示市場尚未形成穩
定回升趨勢。我們認為，當前股市面臨的不僅是短期賣壓，更包括國際投資人信任下
降及風險溢價提高，即使企業基本面未同步惡化，仍可能因評價下修而承受股價壓力

。因此，短線不排除政策利多或跌深後出現技術性反彈，但在 MSCI 態度、外資流向
與印尼盾走勢明顯改善前，反彈延續性仍有疑慮，印尼股市中期展望維持保守。
四、市場展望：
1. 印尼股市維持保守展望，單一市場風險報酬吸引力不足：考量 MSCI 降評風險尚未解除、
外資撤離壓力持續 ， 以及市場治理改革仍有待驗證 ， 我們認為印尼股市未來數季的風險報
酬吸引力相對有限。即使近期股價明顯回落，仍難認定相關風險已充分反映。相較市場制
度成熟、國際資金流動性較佳的新興市場，印尼股市可能持續面臨較高風險溢價，因此對
單一印尼市場的集中曝險應維持審慎態度。
2. 此事件對新興市場指數與東協指數影響相對有限：根據 MSCI 資料，截至2026 年 9 月底
，印尼股市在 MSCI Emerging Market 指數與 MSCI AC ASEAN 指數中的權重分別約為
0.43%與 7.55%，因此我們認為印尼潛在降評對相關指數的直接衝擊相對有限。此外，新
興市場各國經濟結構與產業組成有所差異 ， 區域型基金可透過跨國分散降低單一市場風險

。惟後續仍須留意印尼事件對整體新興市場投資信心造成的外溢影響 ， 以及全球貿易與匯
率波動風險。

AI 資本支出侵蝕現金流，科技巨頭財報拖累科技股 
                                   2026 年 10 月 30 日 
一、 市場動態 
1. AI 資本支出與現金流疑慮再起，那斯達克領跌：美股 10 月 29 日主要指數收黑，S&P 
500 下跌 0.30%、Nasdaq 下跌 1.38%、 道瓊下跌0.25%、 費半下跌1.42%， 費半ETF
盤後跌幅進一步擴大至逾 3%。 市場下跌主要因Alphabet 財報營收與獲利低於預期、
搜尋廣告業務成長放緩，加上大幅增加資本支出導致單季自由現金流轉負，股價重挫
12.64%，再度引發市場對 AI 投資報酬的疑慮，賣壓亦擴及半導體及軟體類股。其他
盤後公布財報的科技公司表現亦出現雜音，Amazon 資本支出明顯高於預期，近 12
個月自由現金流轉為淨流出，Intel 則因 PC 需求疲弱下修財測，使科技股盤後賣壓進
一步擴大。不過，當日 S&P 500 約 81%成分股上漲，顯示市場賣壓主要集中於 AI 相
關大型科技股，而非整體市場同步走弱。 
2. Fed 按兵不動但兩票主張升息，12 月升息機率仍高：Fed 於 10 月 28 日以 10 比 2 維
持利率於 3.75%～4.00%，Cook 與 Hammack 主張升息 1 碼，期貨市場隱含 12 月
升息機率約 85%。9 月 PCE 年增 3.6%，10 年期美債殖利率在 5%附近震盪。 
3. 中東情勢緊張 ， 油價維持百元以上高檔 ：布蘭特原油 10 月 28 日升至每桶 115 美元附
近，WTI 亦站上 102 美元。高油價增加通膨與 Fed 升息壓力，也推升 AI 資料中心電
力成本，持續壓抑科技股評價。

二、 大型科技企業財報重點： 
1. 雲端業務全面加速，AI 需求仍未降溫：本週公布財報的大型科技企業中，雲端業務成
長普遍加速。Alphabet 旗下 Google Cloud 營收 307.09 億美元、年增 103%，TPU
系統銷售成為重要動能；Microsoft Azure 年增 48%，下季財測進一步加速至 49～
50%；Amazon AWS 營收 459.8 億美元、年增 39.3%，為近五年最快增速。Intel 資
料中心與 AI 營收亦年增 78%，創 15 年來最強成長。各家均表示算力需求仍高於可用
供給，顯示 AI 基礎建設需求尚未出現降溫跡象。 
2. 資本支出持續攀升，自由現金流表現決定股價分歧：Microsoft 於 10 月 28 日盤後公
布 2027 會計年度第一季財報，營收 922.79 億美元、年增 18.8%，營業利益率達
49.8%，自由現金流亦維持正值，整體財報優於市場預期。相較之下，Alphabet 雲端
業務表現亮眼，但廣告業務成長未達預期，且單季資本支出達 513.55 億美元，導致
自由現金流轉為負 90.65 億美元，股價於 10 月 29 日重挫 12.64%；Amazon 單季資
本支出則達 773 億美元，近 12 個月自由現金流轉為淨流出 424 億美元，加上第四季
營收財測中值低於預期 ， 盤後股價下跌逾6%。 資本支出指引方面 ，Alphabet 維持 2026
年 1,950 億至 2,050 億美元，Amazon 維持約 2,200 億美元，Microsoft 則因第三方

3. PC 需求疲弱拖累 Intel 財測，Oracle 明確籌資路徑緩解融資疑慮：Intel 第三季營收
165 億美元、年增 21%，non-GAAP EPS 0.52 美元優於預期，但記憶體等零組件成
本上升壓抑 PC 需求，客戶端運算營收年減5%，第四季營收財測 150～160 億美元低
於市場預期約 171 億美元，盤後股價重挫逾 20%。另一方面，Oracle 於 10 月 28 日
投資人日設定 FY2030 營收目標逾 2,250 億美元，並承諾 FY2030 前額外外部籌資上

限為 200 億美元（股、債各 100 億美元） 、自由現金流於FY2028 下半年轉正，股價
當日大漲約 11%，有助降低市場對 AI 基礎建設融資風險的擔憂（詳見圖 2）。
圖 2：Intel 財報與 Oracle 投資人日重點
公司 關鍵數據 市場關注重點

營收 165 億美元（+21%）；DCAI 73 億美元（+78%）；

三、 市場看法：
1. 市場不再單純獎勵 AI 投資，開始要求可驗證的財務報酬：Microsoft 與 Alphabet 同

樣大舉投資 AI， 股價反應卻明顯分歧 ， 關鍵在於AI 投資是否已轉化為獲利與現金流。
我們認為，市場評價標準已由「是否投資AI」轉向「AI 投資能否帶來足夠的營收成長
與現金流」 ， 具備穩定現金流與明確商業化能力的企業將相對受青睞 ， 而自由現金流持
續為負、高度依賴外部融資的業者則可能面臨評價壓縮。

不另行通知。
2. 雲端未認列合約持續擴大 ，AI 需求尚未出現結構性反轉 ：Microsoft 商業 RPO 達 6,770
億美元、年增 73%，Oracle RPO 約 6,640 億美元，AWS 與 Google Cloud backlog
亦分別達 5,780 億及 5,610 億美元，四家合計約 2.48 兆美元（詳見圖 3） 。這顯示目
前市場疑慮主要在於投資回收期與資金壓力，而非終端需求惡化；只要雲端業者持續
擴充算力，先進製程、伺服器與資料中心供應鏈中期仍具支撐。
3. 資本支出 2027 年仍將成長，台灣 AI 供應鏈持續受惠：四大雲端／平台業者 2026 年

資本支出合計約 7,200～7,400 億美元 ， 且2027 年仍將增加 。 元大投顧認為 ，Google
擴大 TPU 對外銷售及 Microsoft 持續部署 GPU 與自研 Maia 加速器，將帶動伺服器
ODM、 散熱及機殼需求 ， 受惠供應鏈包括廣達 、 奇鋐 、 雙鴻 、 健策 、 富世達及聯發科。
融資面則宜留意 AI 相關企業公司債利差及再融資條件變化。
4. Fed 鷹派立場限制評價空間，短線震盪可能延續：12 月升息預期升溫、通膨與油價維
持高檔，使 10 年期美債殖利率停留在 5%附近，壓抑高評價科技股評價擴張。只要
AI 需求未出現結構性反轉，維持短線審慎，對科技股中期偏正向看法。
圖 3：主要雲端業者未認列合約（Backlog／RPO）規模（單位：億美元）
公司 未認列合約規模 相關說明

四、市場展望：
1. 科技股短線波動較大，中期看好現金流穩健的 AI 受惠企業：本週財報顯示雲端營收加速
成長與未認列合約持續擴大，AI 需求仍強；但資本支出攀升、自由現金流轉弱，加上Fed
可能於 12 月升息，短線科技股波動恐將延續。中期而言，AI 產業將由全面成長轉向基本
面分化，具技術優勢、財務結構穩健且 AI 投資已能轉化為獲利的企業，如雲端與 AI 軟體
平台領先者，以及先進製程、伺服器、散熱等關鍵供應鏈，風險報酬相對有利；對高度依
賴外部融資、自由現金流持續為負的企業則宜維持審慎，並留意零組件成本上升對 PC 等
消費性電子需求的排擠效應。
2. 美債避險功能仍在，中天期債券風險報酬相對均衡：10 月 29 日科技股下跌時，10 年期

美債殖利率由約 5.05%回落至約 4.99%，顯示美債仍具一定避險效果。惟 Fed 鷹派立場
及油價高檔使短天期公債易受升息預期影響 ， 超長天期公債則面臨財政赤字與期限溢價壓
力，中天期公債風險報酬相對均衡。投資級債中期維持偏正向；高收益債則須留意 AI 相
關發行人自由現金流轉負及利差擴大風險。

者確認資本支出持續成長後，下週財報將進一步檢驗 AI 供應鏈訂單能否轉化為營收與獲
利（詳見圖 4）。

資料中心監管風暴衝擊 AI 供應鏈，美股全面重挫
2026 年 11 月 06 日
一、 市場動態
1. 資料中心監管疑慮引爆 AI 供應鏈賣壓，美股選後漲勢一日反轉：民主黨取得參、眾兩
院多數，美國進入分裂政府格局。S&P 500 於 11 月 4 日先漲 1.58%，11 月 5 日卻重
挫 4.36%，創2025 年 4 月以來最大單日跌幅 ；Nasdaq 跌 5.52%，費半大跌 12.25%，
十一大產業全數收黑。主因有三：一是資料中心開發成為州級政策議題，德州州長當
選人主張暫停新建，全美新增容量約三成可能受影響；二是高通與 Arm 財報不如預

期；三是建設遞延將推高依賴外部融資業者的持有成本。費半重挫前已自 6 月高點回
落近三成，政策不確定性進一步放大跌幅（詳見表一）。
表一：美股主要指數表現（2026/11/05 收盤）
主要指數 收盤點位 單日漲跌幅
S&P 500 指數 7,353.73 −4.36%
Nasdaq 綜合指數 24,315.00 −5.52%
費城半導體指數 9,011.14 −12.25%
資料來源：Reuters、Nasdaq、元大銀行整理
2. 波動率指數急升，公債殖利率全面走低，油價反彈：11 月 5 日避險情緒升溫，VIX 盤
中升至 28.09（前日 16.61）。資金湧入美債，2 年、5 年、10 年及 30 年期殖利率分
別降至 4.46%、4.59%、4.84%及 5.25%，呈牛市陡峭化；12 月升息一碼機率由約
83%降至約 65%。布蘭特原油反彈 3.88%至每桶 98.29 美元，通膨前景仍具不確定性
（詳見表二） 。

二、 下跌原因
1. 民主黨橫掃國會及州長選舉，資料中心開發成為州級政策議題：德州州長當選人 Gina
Hinojosa 主張暫停新建資料中心，俄亥俄州與喬治亞州當選人也提出限制。地方政府

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
可透過電價分攤、建設許可及稅務優惠影響新案進度，UBS 估計 2028 年全美新增資
料中心容量約三成可能受影響。國會對科技巨頭與資料中心用電的監督亦可能增強，
半導體、電力設備及資料中心類股成為賣壓重心。
2. 高通手機晶片業績低於預期，加重半導體類股賣壓：高通財年第四季調整後 EPS 年減
28%至 2.17 美元，手機晶片營收年減 26%至 51.37 億美元，低於預期，主因蘋果相
關收入減少；汽車與物聯網營收雖年增 36%，仍難抵銷缺口，股價隔日收跌 9.5%，
進一步放大半導體跌幅。
3. 融資成本與投資回收壓力加重，依賴外部融資業者首當其衝：資料中心資本支出多發

生於上線前，若電力、許可或工程延後，收入認列隨之推遲，利息與持有成本卻須持
續負擔。儘管 12 月升息機率降至約 65%，借款成本仍高，依賴外部融資或單一客戶
合約的 AI 基礎建設業者將面臨回報壓縮與再融資風險。
4. 科技股在本次重挫前已明顯修正 ， 評價落至極低水準：費半自 6 月 22 日高點 14,634.72
點已回落逾 30%，Nasdaq 亦自 10 月 6 日高點 27,599.89 點回落逾 10%。市場質疑
AI 投資效益，降低對高評價企業的容忍度，政策不確定性因而放大修正。近期跌幅較
屬估值下調而非基本面惡化，若晶片訂單與獲利預估維持穩定，市場有望回溫。
表三：11/5 主要科技股及類股表現
標的 單日漲跌幅 重點說明
費城半導體指數 −12.25% 選後跌幅最深，AI 供應鏈評價重估
高通（Qualcomm） −9.5% 手機晶片營收年減 26%，低於預期

三、 市場看法
1. 短期評價修正而非長期趨勢反轉，AI 資本循環仍處擴張階段：我們視本波為多頭格局
中的健康修正。修正反映股東對龐大資本承諾要求更高風險補償，而非 AI 變現失速：
Google Cloud 第三季營收年增 103%、 營業利益年增209%，四大雲端業者資本支出
預估上修至約 1.16 兆美元，顯示企業看見更大市場而主動加碼（詳見表四）。此外，
政策衝擊集中於州與地方，聯邦仍支持資料中心擴建，選舉結果本質是要求用電成本
內部化，而非否定 AI 投資。
表四：AI 基本面持續擴張──關鍵指標一覽
指標 最新數據 解讀
Google Cloud 第三季營收 年增 103% AI 需求快速轉化為收入
Google Cloud 第三季營業利益 年增 209% 獲利能力未因擴張而惡化
四大雲端業者資本支出預估 約 8,732 億→1.16 兆美元 企業主動加碼，投資循環仍擴張
資料來源：元大投顧、Bloomberg；元大銀行整理

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。 
2. 留意五大反彈訊號，利空逐步消化後市場可望回穩：（一）州級電價與併網制度若於
2027 年上半年 建立 ，延後 專案可望 下半年重新加速 ，眾議院 以 417 比 3 通過
《Ratepayer Protection Act》亦顯示跨黨派共識；（二）雲端業者資本支出指引未下
修、未見取消訂單；（三）11 月 10 日 CPI 及 13 日 PPI 物價月增率回落；（四）財政
部維持附息債標售規模，資金回流市場；（五）VIX 回落且投資等級債利差穩定。訊號
出現後，市場有望回歸基本面。 
3. 政策先影響建設速度，AI 需求仍須以訂單驗證：州級限制首先改變的是資料中心選址
與交付時程，而非 AI 需求本身。雲端客戶若維持支出計畫，專案可調整順序或轉往他
州，惟設備交付遞延將壓抑供應商近季營收；聯邦立法限制則須經國會及總統否決權，
短期落地難度高。若主要客戶訂單穩定，本次修正較屬建設時程遞延的風險重定價。 
4. 新增算力面臨遞延，既有算力相對有利：高盛估計可能收緊開發限制的州約占美國規
劃中資料中心容量的 45%，其中德州約占 25%。新案放慢下，大型雲端業者既有算力
可望受惠於利用率與議價能力提升，資本支出延後亦減輕現金流壓力；仍待併網的專
案及高槓桿業者則面臨回收遞延，產業鏈分化將擴大。 
5. 歷史經驗顯示選後股市多正向，後續表現仍宜留意基本面變化：彭博統計 1950 年以
來，美國期中選舉後 12 個月 S&P 500 平均報酬約 15%，19 次全數收正，最高為 1954
年的逾 30%，最弱的 1986 年亦小幅上漲。府會分治抑制政策大幅轉向為選後行情主
要支撐，惟後續表現仍須觀察基本面(詳見下圖)。

6. 企業獲利仍強勁成長，本次修正以估值與風險溢價重估為主：彭博 11 月 4 日統計，
S&P 500 已公布財報公司中 79.85%優於預期，資訊科技更達 92.45%，第三季 EPS
成長率分別為 29.0%及 67.8%（詳見表五）。財報為美股提供中期支撐，短期評價則
取決於 2027 年獲利預估能否承受建設遞延與融資成本壓力。

7. 最新經濟數據仍具韌性，聯準會利率路徑仍具變數：初領失業救濟金降至 18.9 萬人，
第三季勞動生產力季增年率 3.6%、單位勞動成本季減 0.7%，經濟仍具韌性。惟油價
逼近每桶 100 美元，若通膨高於預期，升息定價恐再升溫，壓抑科技股估值與長債價
格。
8. 信用市場是判斷風險是否由股權評價擴散至金融條件的重要指標：若 AI 企業持續擴大
資本支出與外部融資，償債能力將受關注。大型投資等級科技企業現金流穩定，較能

吸收壓力；高槓桿或依賴單一客戶的 AI 基礎建設業者則較易受利差上升衝擊。信用利
差若維持穩定，風險仍以股權評價調整為主；若同步擴大，則須警惕金融條件收緊。
四、 投資建議：
1. 短期修正不改 AI 長期趨勢 ， 切勿恐慌殺低錯殺優質標的：本次重挫源自選後政策不確
定性與評價重新定價，AI 需求與企業獲利尚未反轉，屬多頭中的短期波動。應避免恐
慌出清而錯殺優質標的；已持有者以續抱為主，加碼宜分批逢低，避免高槓桿操作。
2. 以股債平衡穿越短期波動，等待基本面重新主導市場：我們認為，本次修正屬短期政
策衝擊與評價調整，中長期科技股展望仍正面。在政策與通膨明朗前，建議將集中度
過高的科技股部位，適度轉向波動較低的平衡型或多重資產商品，搭配優質債券穩定
收益，並保留股票部位分批布局優質標的，待反彈訊號確認後再提高風險資產比重。
3. 股票部位聚焦優質、評價回落且前景較佳的產業：修正後部分優質企業評價已回到合
理區間，股票建議聚焦：一、握有長期電力合約、已通電園區的大型雲端業者；二、
明年展望佳的 AI 供應鏈，如 ASIC、光通訊、電力設備、半導體設備及資安；三、跌

幅較小的軟體及非科技優質股。高度仰賴新建資料中心與外部融資的業者，宜待環境
明朗再評估。
4. 中天期美國公債及高信評投資等級債仍較具優勢：在美國政策風險升溫及科技股評價
調整期間，美國公債仍具投資組合避險功能，但考量通膨與能源價格的不確定性，現
階段不建議延長存續期間。中天期美國公債及高信評投資等級債在兼顧票息收益、利
率敏感度與資產配置穩定性上仍較具優勢。信用債方面，營運現金流充足、槓桿適中
及再融資需求可控的發行人仍較為穩健，對AI 相關企業則須進一步評估資本支出、合
約現金流與債務到期結構。

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: MediaTek (MTK) disclosed on 30 October a strong set of 
quarterly results and guidance both beating market expectations, thanks to 
a faster-than-expected ramp in its first AI accelerator project. We walked 
away from today’s call with a firmer view on its AI business - a faster ramp 
for the first project and a broadened customer base for a new project. We 
raise our forecasts and 12M TP, reiterate our Buy (1) call and suggest that 
investors take advantage of the recent stock pullbacks for bargain-hunting.

What’s the impact: Results and guidance all beat. MTK released 3Q26 
net profit of c.TWD28bn, beating our/consensus estimates by 11%/9% 
thanks to a better topline, lower opex ratio and higher non-operating gains 
(table on p.2). 3Q26 revenue finished 6%/4% above our/consensus 
estimates and topped guidance (TWD152-160bn), thanks to an earlier 
ramp of its first AI accelerators on some easing of the substrate supply 
constraint. Guidance calls for 4Q26 revenue of TWD216bn, at the midpoint 
of the range that beat our previous/consensus forecasts by 7%/3%, driven 
by the stronger ramp of the AI accelerator project which will more than 
offset muted consumer demand weakness associated with mobile phones. 
We raise our 2026-28E EPS by 1-6% and expect its AI revenue to reach 
USD16bn in 2027 and USD26bn in 2028, from USD2.7bn this year.

AI appears to be running ahead. At today’s call, management centred 
elaboration on its AI business progress. 1) It raised this year’s initial 
revenue contribution of the first AI accelerators to USD2.5bn for 4Q26, 
above its previous target of USD2bn. 2) While management keeps its AI 
accelerator (ex-HBM) addressable market unchanged at USD80bn for 
2027, it now aims for a 20% market share (previous: 15-20%), meaning 
USD16bn revenue contribution, vs. our previous forecast of USD15bn. 3) It 
expects revenue contribution in 2028 from the second AI customer (not 
from China), which supports our view depicted in our Global Tech report 
issued on 27 August, where we lifted MTK’s 2028 forecasts on the second 
customer ramp. 4) MTK aims to kick off 4Q26 with a new journey for its 
operating margin, expanding all the way into 2027 for more than 20% on 
average, echoing its previous comments that, although the AI accelerator 
business is gross-margin dilutive, it is operating-margin accretive.

What we recommend: Given our forecast revisions, we finetune our 12M 
TP for MTK to TWD5,500 (previous: TWD5,200), based on our unchanged 
4-quarter forward PER of 36x. We reiterate our Buy (1) rating with key 
downside risk of any AI accelerator ramps below our expectation.

How we differ: Our 2026-27E EPS are 6-8% above the consensus 
forecasts as we believe the street has yet to factor in its better AI ramp.

30 October 2026

Firmly on track with AI ASIC transformer

Company profile 
Ranked No.5 in the world by 2023 fabless semiconductor revenue, MediaTek (MTK) offers diverse 
products across optical storage, digital home, smartphone, PC, ASIC, automotive and other IoT 
applications. MTK has successfully captured the industry demand cycles from optical disk drives to 
feature phones to smartphones, and now positions itself well as a platform provider to capitalise on the 
next demand cycle of the BigData/AI to help facilitate digital transformation in human lives.

### Page 5

5

MediaTek (2454 TT): 30 October 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 4 Feb 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
MTK’s Board of Directors (BoD) has 8 members with diverse backgrounds in foundry, IC design
and electronics. Among its BoD, 4 members are independent directors, representing 50% of total
directors. Its Chairman and CEO are not the same person, but its CEO is also one of the
directors. MTK conducts internal annual performance evaluation of the BoD, individual directors
and functional committees.
The board currently includes only one female director out of eight members, which represents
12.5% of the total. The company recognizes this gender imbalance and is actively working
toward a more inclusive and balanced board composition
Capital management 2
MTK’s dividend payout ratios in the past 5 years were above 60% with increasing cash dividend
in the past 3 years in dollar amount. In addition, MTK is committed to paying a special dividend
of TWD16/share over 2021-24 on top of its regular annual dividend. MTK is one of the leading
fabless chipmakers in the world with continued efforts in new product development for diversified
applications; therefore, it will continue to invest in its research & development. We see its
dividend payout as appropriate.
Related party &
transaction 1
MTK’s revenue from related parties was less than 1% of its total revenue in 2024 and transaction
terms were not abnormal compared to other customers. It also had related-party transactions
with King Yuan Electronics Co., Ltd. and King Long Tech (Suzhou) Ltd for IC testing,
experimental services, and manufacturing technology services in 2021. The purchase amount
from related parties was less than 7% of its total cost of goods sold/purchases in 2024. Thus, we
do not see meaningful impact on its business operation.
S Supply chain management 2
MTK has established its "Supplier Code of Conduct", which covers areas such as labour and
human rights, health and safety, environmental protection, code of ethics, and management. It
requires all suppliers to sign a guarantee to comply with this code of conduct. MTK implements
an annual review to continuously track the actual compliance of suppliers. We believe these
measures can help MTK reduce supply chain risk.
E Water & wastewater management 1
As a fabless without manufacturing plants, MTK does not produce much wastewater compared
to manufacturing companies, and thus has relatively limited impact on the environment.
MTK has initiated water conservation methods, including efficient enhancement of using water-
saving apparatuses, installing rainwater recycling tanks and using recycled water in cooling
towers.
E
Waste & hazardous
materials
management
1
MTK prioritises waste reduction, classification for reuse, adherence to recycling, and reutilisation
to manage waste efficiently and maximise the benefits of recycling. Also, it chooses only
qualified partners for waste disposal and recycling, and also audits the waste processes
regularly to ensure legal disposal of its waste and to fulfil its responsibility in waste management
supervision.
E GHG emissions 1
The main source for MTK’s GHG emissions is externally purchased electricity, which accounts
for over 90% of its total emissions. To enhance energy efficiency and reduce emissions, the
company implemented several measures, including ongoing improvements to data center
efficiency, full adoption of LED lighting in offices, and optimized operation of chiller units.
MediaTek continued advancing its renewable energy strategy and alignment with global climate
initiatives such as SBT and RE100. In early 2025, the Company passed the SBTi review and set
targets based on a 2020 baseline, including a 40% reduction in Scope 1 and 2 emissions by
2030, net zero by 2050, and achieving 100% renewable energy use across global offices
(excluding data centers) by 2030.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

VPEC (2455.TW): Optoelectronics mix upgrade driving GM expansion; 
3Q26 in line; Buy
2 November 2026 | 5:30AM HKT
3Q26 revenues increased 39% QoQ, with optoelectronics (mainly InP epiwafer for PD 
and CW lasers) revenues contribution up to 43% (vs. 27% in 2Q26), driving GM up to 
45% (vs. 39% in 2Q26). Along with opex ratio further improved to 13.7% (vs. 15.4% 
in 2Q26), VPEC’s OP income increased 87% QoQ in 3Q26, or 2% behind / 7% above 
our / Bloomberg consensus estimates. Looking ahead, we expect 4Q26E revenues to 
further increase by 14% QoQ, with GM up to 46% driven by product mix upgrade 
toward Optoelectronics. The company also announced its issuance of unsecured 
convertible bonds valued at NT$1.5bn (US$46m), to supplement its procurement in 
raw materials and equipment, reflecting the strong demand and VPEC’s commitment 
in capacity expansion; as we highlighted in Oct (Global Server TAM updates) where 
our US Internet team forecast that leading US cloud service providers (CSPs) in 
aggregate would grow their capex budgets by 84%/ 52%/ 13% YoY in 2026E-28E 
and where we raise our estimates by 4% / 17% / 22% in 2026-28E to US$1,397bn by 
2028E. We remain positive view on VPEC, which we believe is positioned to benefit 
from the AI server racks ramp up, optical networking specification upgrades across 
transmitters / CW lasers (toward 1.6T optical modules) and receivers (toward 200G 
PD), product expansion toward EML base epiwafers, further customer penetration 
for CW lasers, improving InP substrate supply, and opportunities in InP epiwafers for 
high power lasers for NPO and CPO. Maintain Buy.

YoY in 3Q26; Looking ahead, we expect +14% QoQ in 4Q26E, riding on new AI server 
racks ramp up and optical networking specification upgrade toward 1.6T optical

Valuation: We continue to derive our 12m target price from 2029E P/E discounted back 
to 2027E at 10.5% COE (no change), and derive our target P/E multiple from refreshed 
peers’ trading P/E vs. forward year fundamentals (NI YoY and OPM). Based on our 
2029E-30E NI YoY of 18% (unchanged) and 2029-30E OPM of 38% (unchanged), and 
peers’ PEG&M of 0.4x (unchanged), we derive target P/E multiple at 22.0x (vs. 22.2x 
previously), reflecting a slight peer group multiple compression amid the product 
transition window from 800G to 1.6T optical transceivers, balanced by sustained 
long-term AI optical networking demand. Our new 12-month target price is at NT$785 
(vs. NT$793 previously). Our new TP implies 2027E P/E at 68.7x (vs. 69.4x previously), 
within the company’s trading range (vs. peak at 74x, and +1stv. at 47x). Maintain Buy.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

AirTAC International  | Asia Pacific
October Sales -6% MoM/+32%
YoY
Here we focus on AirTAC's monthly sales, which we believe
could be a catalyst for its share price.
What's new:
• October sales came in at NT$3,565mn (-6% MoM/+32% YoY), mainly on 15%
fewer working days, as daily shipments were up 7% MoM.
• By segment, shipments of machine tools (+31% YoY), electronics (+30%
YoY), batteries (+27% YoY), machinery (+25% YoY) and textiles (+25% YoY)

outperformed during the month.
• QTD revenue reached 33% of our estimate at NT$10,918mn (-5% QoQ/+21%
YoY) and 32% of consensus at NT$11,228mn (-2% QoQ/+24% YoY).
Outlook:
• We think 4Q26 revenue is tracking in line with management guidance of
roughly flat QoQ – better than the typical seasonal decline.
• Management remains positive on the growth outlook, driven by share gains
alongside cyclical upturn tailwinds and government policy support.
• We remain OW, as the current valuation of 15x 2027e P/E is undemanding vs.

its mid-cycle average of 25x.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
AirTAC International (1590.TW)
Base case, 29x 2027e P/E. We believe this methodology better captures earnings growth
momentum for industrial automation component plays (market sentiment moves along
with business cycles). As the industry enters an up-cycle, we think applying a near-peak cycle
valuation of 29x P/E is reasonable given the stronger business outlook.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger macroeconomic climate in China

n Stronger-than-expected demand from industrial automation
Risks to Downside
n Sharper economic downturn in China
n Miniature linear guideway business development taking more time
n Larger-than-expected investment in new products but weaker-than-expected demand

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 1

M
Idea
Wiwynn Corp  | Asia Pacific
3Q beat on higher GM/OpM
Recent concerns revolve around potential data center delays,
though the supply chain has not seen any changes. However, we
lower our mid-term growth  rate to bake in some conservatism.
Our PT now implies a lower CY27 P/E at  12x. RR looks attractive
at 7.7x CY27 P/E vs. the 3Y  avg. of ~10x.
4Q revenue should continue to grow double-digits q/q: Wiwynn announced
October revenue at ~NT$165B (-4% m/m, +81% y/y). If we assume the October

monthly run-rate can be sustained, 4Q should grow at least 13-14% q/q. However,
we think ASIC server racks will continue to ramp-up through-out year-end, so we
forecast 4Q revenue to ~20% q/q to reach ~NT$520B (+78% y/y). Higher cost
structure and higher mix of ASIC server racks should drive margins to decline q/q to
~8.5% (-40bps q/q), resulting in net profit of ~NT$27.7B or EPS of NT$46.70.
ASIC/GPU projects remain on track: Our checks indicate that  Trainium3 server rack

production is ramping up well at Wiwynn, and with T3, Wiwynn is a more
comprehensive partner, also engaging in UBB and switch trays. It will also engage in
AMD's (covered by Joe Moore) MI455 Helios rack assembly for customers such as
Meta (covered by Brian Nowak) and Oracle (covered by Sanjit Singh). We forecast
Wiwynn to build ~8K Helios racks for CY27.
3Q results beat on better margins: Revenue was NT$434.6B (+63% y/y) with GM
of 8.9% (-40bps q/q), which is 90bps/30bps above MSe/Street. OP reached ~NT

$32.5B (+66% y/y) for OpM of 7.5% (+20bps q/q), which is 100bps/50bps above
MSe/Street, driven by operating leverage. Non-op losses were slightly higher than
MSe/Street mainly due to higher interest expense, resulting in net profit of ~NT
$23.4B (+52% y/y) for EPS of NT$42.09, 44%/10% above MSe/Street.
Reiterate OW with price target unchanged at NT$2,515 (12x/11x CY27/28e P/E) –
our higher EPS estimates are offset by a lower mid-term growth rate. Risk-reward is

highly attractive at 7.7x CY27e or 6.7x CY28e P/E vs. the three-year range of 6-16x,
with a strong pipeline of AI and non-AI server projects.

Key Takeaways
3Q EPS was NT$42.09, 44% above our estimate/10% above the Street, driven by
GM/OpM beat despite higher non-op losses.

Oct rev at ~NT$165M (+81% y/y), implies 4Q rev should grow double-digits q/q,
driven by continued strength from general server and rising ASIC server racks.

Our overall earnings estimate changes
We raise our EPS estimates 13% for CY26, 29% for CY27, and 29% for CY28: This
mainly reflects changes to our assumptions, which we laid out in the section above. The
difference between net profit and EPS change is due to changes in share count.

### Page 6

M
Idea
6
Price target discussion and valuation methodology
We keep our price target unchanged at NT$2,515: This implies 12x CY27e P/E or 11x CY28
P/E, vs. its thee-year average forward P/E of 10.3x, which we think is fair, in view of
Wiwynn's AI server project wins and share gains for general-purpose servers in the next 1-
2 years.
We continue to use a residual income model to derive our price target. Key assumptions
include a cost of equity of 7.0% (beta of 1.0, equity premium of 6% and risk-free rate of

1%), a medium-term growth rate of 4% (lowered from 6%), and a terminal growth rate of
3%.
Our bull and bear case scenario values are also unchanged at NT$3,210 and NT$1,470,
respectively.

MS Rating
▪ Beneﬁciary of enlarged CPU TAM driven

by rising demand for agentic AI.
▪ Share gainer within ASIC programs, with
Wiwynn taking share on UBB and switch
tray assembly, along with ramp up of
Trainium 3 from 2H26.
▪ Wiwynn will also be the main ODM for
Meta and Oracle's AMD MI4505 Helios
server racks, which will mainly contribute in
CY27.
▪ It will also be a supplier of Nvidia VR200
racks for Oracle, which will mainly
contribute in CY27.
▪ Our price target implies 12x CY27e P/E,
not stretched vs. the three-year average of

~10x.

16x 2027e P/E
Data center builds accelerate: Assumes

NT$2,515.00
12x 2027e P/E
Based on our analysis, we now expect AI
servers to account for ~50% of revenue in
2027, from 20-30% in 2024, as Wiwynn
ramps up its AI server shipments and
general server demand also grows strongly.
NT$1,470.00
7.3x 2027e P/E
Data center builds get pushed out: Assumes
geopolitical tensions escalate, with macro
data deteriorating, driving hyperscale

vendors such as Meta and Microsoft to push
out their data center builds, which leads to
further demand downside for both general
computing servers and AI servers.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

研 究  報 告                
聯亞(3081.TT) 
產業：電子–通信網路 
矽光需求持續強勁，更多設備投產與新增後段外包夥伴帶動產能倍增  
 投資評價與建議 
受惠CW Laser出貨量持續增加、新購顯影設備逐步進駐以及後段代工廠產能
開出，預估稅後EPS為21.01/49.3/97.15元，考量NPO和CPO技術逐步成熟且
聯亞具備多波⻑雷射領先技術，從2028年獲利評價，給予40倍本益比。 
 重點評論分析 
矽光產品出貨增加 ， 3Q26營收成⻑ ， 主要客⼾已下⻑期訂單 ， 4Q26營收攀升 ：
3Q26營收季增27.9%至15.6億元 ， 係因矽光產品出貨增加 ， 毛利率因營收規模
擴大上升1.11ppts至58.6%，營業費用較2Q26增加約1,400萬元至1.43億元，
主要來自研發費用以及隨獲利增加按比例提列之員工分紅費用，稅後EPS為
6.12元。4Q26矽光產品需求持續強勁，主要客戶皆已開始下6個⽉以上的⻑期
訂單，訂單能見度佳，隨公司本身與下游代工廠產能逐步開出，預期矽光產品
出貨量逐季成⻑，預估4Q26營收季增23.5%至19.3億元，稅後EPS為7.19元。  
設備與後段代工產能開出， 2027和2028年總產量連續兩年皆年增 150%：展
望2027~2028年，1.6T矽光產品已量產，3.2T與NPO/CPO應用之更高功率矽
光產品則和客戶持續合作開發和驗證中 ， 針對特定客戶之資料中心200G PD已
完成驗證且磊晶產能已規劃，預計未來數⽉將開始量產。目前E-Beam、奈米
壓印技術與DUV深紫外光微影三種技術皆同步進行擴產，隨更多E-Beam投
產，1Q27產能將明顯增加，奈米壓印設備預計於2026年底進廠。 
 投資風險 
設備與後段產能開出進度不如預期。

### Page 2

2

| 營運概況  
矽光產品出貨持續增加與後段產能開出，3Q26營收成⻑，主要客⼾已下⻑期訂單，4Q26營收持續攀升： 3Q26營收季
增27.9%至15.6億元 ， 係因矽光產品出貨量增加 ， 毛利率因營收規模擴大上升1.11ppts至58.6% ， 營業利益為7.71億元，
營業利益率為49.4%，營業費用較2Q26增加約1,400萬元至1.43億元，主要來自研發費用以及隨獲利增加按比例提列之
員工分紅費用，折舊費用較2Q26增加160萬元至7,660萬元，稅後淨利為6.21億元，稅後EPS為6.12元。4Q26矽光產品
需求持續強勁，主要客戶皆已開始下6個⽉以上的⻑期訂單，訂單能見度佳，隨公司本身與下游代工廠產能逐步開出，
預期矽光產品出貨量逐季成⻑，預估4Q26營收季增23.5%至19.3億元，稅後EPS為7.19元。影響毛利率因素有三：整體
營收與產出、匯率變化以及成本。管理層預期營收於接下來幾季將逐季成⻑，惟3Q26前半段台幣升值，隨後9⽉底台幣
轉貶值，匯率變化對3Q26毛利率產生些許不利影響，加上更多人員召募以及設備折舊成本逐步反映，且產品訂價方面，
聯亞僅維持賺取合理利潤，將無法持續大幅漲價，導致毛利率於接下來幾季成⻑幅度將趨緩。 
設備與後段代工產能開出，2027 和 2028 年總產量連續兩年皆年增 150%：展望 2027~2028 年，聯亞 1.6T 矽光產品
已經開始量產，3.2T 與 NPO/CPO 應用之更高功率矽光產品則和客戶持續合作開發和驗證中，另外過去受限產能，公
司設備主要用於生產 LD，自 2027 年開始，針對特定客戶之資料中心 200G 以上規格之 PD 已完成驗證，並已規劃相對
應磊晶產能，將於未來數⽉將開始量產，惟出貨量仍遠小於 LD。2026年 8 ⽉董事會決議與 1 美系資料中心客戶簽訂為
期 4 年之⻑期供貨合約，為滿足該客戶之需求，公司追加約 30 億元資本支出，該資本支出購置之設備可帶動 2027 和
2028 年總產量連續兩年皆年增 150%，另外因應磷化銦基板缺料問題，聯亞目前與 2 家日本主要供應商與 1 家美國主
要供應商達成未來數年的⻑期供貨合約，且美系客戶亦提供部分資金，協助確保後續大規模量產之基板供應無虞。因應
產能不足，聯亞持續新增後段外包合作夥伴位，台灣地區增加數家大型集團作為合作夥伴，該集團在光通訊領域投入意
願高，其資本支出承諾與實力遠高於早期合作夥伴，有助於與聯亞共同爭取國際 AI 訂單。曝光顯影製程方面，目前 E-
Beam、奈米壓印技術與 DUV 深紫外光微影三種技術皆同步進行擴產，首部新購置之 E-Beam 設備已於 2026 年 7 ⽉
開始量產，帶動 7 ⽉營收大幅躍升，隨更多 E-Beam 與 MOCVD 設備投產，1Q27 產能將有明顯增加且未來 8 個季度
產能將逐步增加 ， 奈米壓印設備預計於 2026 年底進場 ， DUV 深紫外光微影設備則於 2027 年以後加入生產 。 綜上所述 ，
預估 2026/2027/2028 年營收年增 155%/130%/86%至56.2/129.1/239.5 億元，稅後 EPS 為 21.01/49.3/97.15 元。

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

두산에너빌리티 (034020)

Company Brief 
유틸리티/건설 
Analyst 문경원, CFA 
kyeongwon.moon@meritz.co.kr 
RA 이동석 
dongseok.lee@meritz.co.kr 3Q26 Review: 바닥을 잡는다면 지금  
믹스 개선에 따른 이익률 개선 조금씩 관찰 중 
3Q26 연결 영업이익은 2,612억원(+90.6% YoY) 을 기록하며 컨센서스(2,455억원)
를 소폭 상회했다. 밥캣(1,674억원)이 컨센서스(1,761억원)를 소폭 하회한 가운데, 
에너빌리티 부문 영업이익(+179% YoY, OPM 6.6%) 이 호실적을 이끌었다. 환율 하
락에도 불구, 가스터빈, 원전 등 고수익성 프로젝트의 비중 상승이 주요 원인이다. 
3Q26 누적 수주는 10.0조원(+85.5% YoY) 을 기록했다. 4Q26 가스복합 EPC 수주 
등을 감안하면 연간 가이던스(13.3조원) 달성은 무난하다. 한수원과의 협상 결렬로 
이목이 집중된 체코 두코바니 시공 수주는 2027년 인식될 확률이 높아 보인다. 주간
사가 바뀌더라도 이미 프로젝트에 참여 중인 동사를 배제하기는 쉽지 않다.

대미투자 이후, 연말~연초 수주 모멘텀에 집중 
연말 연초에 다음과 수주 모멘텀이 집중되면서, 주가 상승을 이끌 전망이다. 
1) 대형원전: 정부 자금 중심의 Westinghouse LLI(장납기 기자재) 발주가 몰린다. 
우선 미국 DOE(에너지부)가 제공한 대출을 기반으로 미국 수주가 기대된다.  폴란드 
정부 역시 최근 LLI 발주 작업을 시작했다. 한편, 한국 정부는 연말까지 100억 달러 
규모의 대미 투자 송금을 협의 중인데, 이 역시 DOC(상무부) 주도의 대형 원전 건설
을 위한 LLI 발주에 쓰일 가능성이 높다 
2) SMR: 오래 기다려왔던 Nuscale 향 수주가 시작되고 있다. 우선 29일 모듈 1기 
제작 계약을 체결했다. 현금이 부족한 Nuscale이 용기를 냈다는 건, 곧 Nuscale-
TVA 간 PPA 가 임박했음을 의미한다. 본격적인 수주는 PPA 직후 예상된다.  
3) 가스 터빈: 2026년 초 해외 수주(7기)는 2029년까지 인도 계획이다. 이후 2030
년 슬랏 예약을 완료하고 2031년 물량을 논의 중으로 보인다. 지금은 2030년 슬랏
에 대한 PO가 나와 수주잔고에 인식할 타이밍이다. 역시 수출 물량으로 추측한다.

성장에 대한 기대가 사라진 밸류에이션, 지금이 바닥 
투자의견 Buy를 유지, 할인율 상승을 반영하여 적정주가를 109,000원으로 -17% 
하향 조정한다. 금리 상승, 환율 하락, 트럼프 지지율 하락 등 Top-down 리스크에 
최근 조정을 받았지만, 추가적인 주가 하락 폭은 제한적일 전망이다. EV/선행 수주잔
고 지표는 현재 1.0배 수준까지 하락하며, 원전의 성장성이 주목받지 못했던 
2021~2024년 수준에 근접하게 내려왔다.  
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 30

Source PDF: all report pdf/[034020] 두산에너빌리티(034020) 펀더멘털은 견고, 노이즈로 인한 일시적 주가 하락두산에너빌리티(034020) 펀더멘털은 견고, 노이즈로 인한_07bab1f0.pdf

### Page 1

(034020) 
두산에너빌리티 
2026.10.30

에너지/인프라/배터리 황성현, Ph.D. 
6878 / tjdgus2009@eugenefn.com

펀더멘털은 견고, 노이즈로 인한 일시적 주가 하락 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 80,000원(하향) 
 현재주가 56,700 원(10/29)

 [투자의견] 당사는 두산에너빌리티에 대해 목표주가를 80,000원(2027년 
BPS의 6.2배)으로 27% 하향하며, 투자의견 BUY를 유지한다. 실적은 견조
한 회복을 보이고 있으나, 최근 GE Vernova(GEV) 등 글로벌 AI 및 에너지 
밸류체인 Peer 주가 조정을 반영해 멀티플을 하향 조정했다. 체코 시공 계
약 등 일부 노이즈에 의해 주가가 급락한 만큼 매수 기회라고 판단한다.  
 [3Q26 Review]  매출액 4.4조원(-5.8%qoq, +14.7%yoy), 영업이익 
2,612억원(-16.9%qoq, +90.6%yoy)을 기록해 당사 추정에 부합하는 무
난한 실적을 기록했다. 에너빌리티 부문은 매출액 1.8조원, 영업이익 1,211
억원(영업이익률 6.6%, +2.3%p qoq)을 기록하며 원자력·가스터빈 중심의 
고수익 사업 믹스 개선으로 분기 수익성이 재차 개선됐다. 당기순이익은 
149억원(-93.4%qoq)에 그쳤으나, 원달러 환율 급락으로 발생한 환손실 
반영 때문으로 이익 체력과는 무관하다고 판단한다. 3Q26 미국 빅테크 데
이터센터향 가스터빈 첫 출하를 달성했으며, 수주잔고는 에너빌리티 부문 
기준 26.6조원(+70.9%yoy)으로 사상 최대치를 경신했다. 
 [4Q26 Preview] 매출액 4.8조원, 영업이익 2,640억원으로 유사한 실적 
흐름을 전망한다. 4Q 가스터빈 인도와 체코 두코바니 원전 진행률 반영으로 
에너빌리티 부문 외형 성장이 기대된다. 3Q26 별도 기준 영업이익률이 
8.5%까지 개선된 만큼 에너빌리티 부문의 분기 실적 개선 추세는 유효하다
고 판단한다. 연간 수주 목표 13.3조원 달성을 위한 잔여 수주(3.3조원 +α)
가 예정되어 있으며, 대형원전을 제외하더라도 가이던스 달성은 무난할 것
이라 판단한다. 세부적으로는 SMR 1조원(계약 조건 합의 완료 단계로 연내 
체결 가능), 신재생 7,000억원(해상풍력 기자재·EPC 2건 협의 완료 단계), 
해외 복합 EPC 5,000억원(국내 1건, 해외 2건)이 기대된다. 
 [가스터빈] 3Q26 누적 가스터빈 13기·스팀터빈 11기·LTSA 6건을 달성하
며 연간 10기 이상 수주·수행 역량이 안정적으로 유지되고 있다. 2030년 
슬롯 예약 물량의 본계약 전환은 2027년, 2031년 슬롯은 2028년으로 예
상되며, 수주잔고 증가가 기대된다. 가스터빈 수요 증가를 감안하면 추가 캐
파 증설 결정이 이르면 연내 이루어질 전망이다. 
 [원전] 10월 NuScale 1개 모듈 제작 계약을 체결했으며, PPA 지연 여부와 
무관하게 완결된 계약으로 실적에 반영될 전망이다. X-energy는 연내 기자
재 및 부품의 후속 계약을 추진 중이다. TerraPower 주기기 3종 중 2종 계
약이 완료되어 제작에 착수했으며, Rolls-Royce SMR·i-SMR 등 파트너십 
확대도 지속되고 있다. 이외에 대미투자 관련 기자재 발주는 미국 부지 확정 
및 한국 의회 승인 절차가 남아있어 내년 발주가 기대된다. 기존 웨스팅하우
스 AP1000 Vogtle 계약 대비 업무 범위 확대(원자로·증기발생기 외 추가 
품목) 가능성이 있어 긍정적으로 평가한다.

시가총액(십억원) 36,320

### Page 2

2_ Eugene Research Center 
설명회 Q&A 
Q1. 4Q26 SMR 수주 목표 약 1 조원의 실현 가능성 
- 고객사와 GTC 등 주요 계약 조건에 대한 합의를 대부분 완료하고 계약 체결 대기 중 
- 연내 계약 가능성이 있어 수주 목표는 의미 있는 수준

Q2. NuScale 모듈 1 기 제작 계약의 PP A 지연 영향 
- 완결된 제작 계약으로 PP A 체결이 지연되더라도 해당 모듈의 제작 및 진행에는 문제없음

Q3. AP1000 장주기 기자재 선발주 후 인허가 지연 시 회계 처리 및 사업 영향 
- DOE·DOC 및 한국 정부 재원을 활용해 EPC 계약·착공 지연과 무관하게 기자재 제작 가능 
- 프로젝트 중단 시 웨스팅하우스의 다른 프로젝트로 기자재 전용 가능성 검토

Q4. 체코 원전 컨소시엄 변경 시 참여 및 사업 계획 
- 현지 지점 운영과 시공인력 상주로 시공 환경 자료 확보 
- 한수원과 초기 인허가·설계에 참여하고, 주기기 공급·설치 역량을 바탕으로 경쟁력 확보 기대

Q5. 체코 원전 EPC 사업의 수익성 확보 가능성 
- 한수원 가이드에 따라 수익성을 고려한 입찰 진행 예정

Q6. 웨스팅하우스 SPV 및 폴란드 AP1000 장주기 기자재 발주 진행 상황 
- DOE·DOC 프로젝트와 폴란드 AP1000 3기 기자재 조기 발주 협의 중 
- 연내 수주 여부는 불확실하나 조기 수주 추진

Q7. 3Q26 영업이익률 개선 요인 
- 원자력·가스터빈 중심의 사업 믹스 개선이 수익성에 기여 
- 제작 진행률과 마일스톤에 따라 이익 인식

### Page 3

두산에너빌리티

Eugene Research Center _3 
Q8. 4Q26 복합화력 수주 목표 5,000억원의 달성 가능성 
- 국내 1 건, 해외 2 건 협의 중이며 국내 프로젝트의 수주 가능성이 높은 상황 
- 대미 투자 물량은 기존 2031년까지의 예약계약 납기와 비교해 검토 중

Q9. 3Q26 에너빌리티부문 당기순손실 원인 
- 급격한 환율 하락에 따른 약 800억원의 환산손실이 주요 원인

Q10. 대미 투자 관련 기자재 발주 시점 
- 미국 부지 확정, 웨스팅하우스 사업제안 및 한국 정부 심사·국회 승인 절차 필요 
- 연말~내년 초 발주 여부는 불확실하나 조기 발주 추진

Q11. 대미 투자 프로젝트의 기자재 공급 범위 확대 가능성 
- 원자로·증기발생기 외 추가 품목까지 공급 범위 확대를 위해 웨스팅하우스와 협상 중

Q12. 장주기 기자재 선발주 후 인허가 지연 시 실적 영향 
- 기자재 출하 시 수금 조건이 충족되며 선발주 자금도 사전 확보 
- 보글·VC 서머 사례에서 기자재 대금을 회수해 프로젝트 지연에 따른 실적 영향은 제한적

Q13. 4Q26 신재생·풍력 수주 목표 약 7,000억원의 달성 가능성 
- 해상풍력 기자재·EPC 계약 및 유지보수 계약 추진 중 
- 주요 계약 협의가 상당 부분 완료돼 일정 지연이 없다면 연내 목표 달성 전망

Q14. 중형 가스터빈 개발 및 해외 진출 계획 
- 대형 가스터빈은 한국·미국 중심으로 추진하며 중동 고객사와도 협의 중 
- 휴스턴 서비스 허브를 기반으로 미국 데이터센터 시장 대응 
- 중형 가스터빈은 시제품 제작 및 시험 진행 후 2028년 하반기 출하 목표

### Page 4

두산에너빌리티

4_ Eugene Research Center 
Q15. NuScale 모듈 1 기 선발주 배경 
- 대규모 일괄 발주에 대비한 제작 경험 확보 및 납기 리스크 완화를 위해 사전 제작 추진

Q16. X-energy 연내 후속 계약 대상 품목 
- 증기발생기 전열관 및 주단 소재 등 장주기 품목의 사전 발주 추진

Q17. DOE 프로젝트의 장주기 기자재 공급 범위 확대 가능성 
- 웨스팅하우스 공급망 상황에 따라 추가 품목 협상 중 
- 과거 보글·VC 서머보다 넓은 공급 범위 기대

Q18. 웨스팅하우스 지분 투자와 기자재 계약 시점의 연계 가능성 
- 지분 투자는 내년, 기자재 계약은 연내 또는 내년 초 목표로 시점이 일치할지는 불확실

Q19. X-energy 장주기 기자재의 소재·부품 구분 
- 증기발생기 전열관은 부품, 주단 소재는 소재에 해당 
- 전열관은 공급 병목에 대비해 조기 발주하고 주단 소재는 설계 확정 후 제작 준비

Q20. 복합화력 주기기 수주의 기존·신규 고객 비중 
- 기존 고객과 추가 예약계약을 협의 중이며 신규 글로벌 고객과도 예약계약 협상 진행

Q21. 주요 SMR 사업 외 파트너십 확대 가능성 
- Rolls-Royce 사업 추진 및 GE Hitachi BWRX-300 주단 소재 예약계약 체결 
- i-SMR은 2035년 운전을 목표로 2028년 이전 발주가 필요해 관련 협의 진행

Q22. X-energy 전열관·주단 소재 수주 물량 규모 
- 다우케미컬 초도 사업은 80MW 원자로 4 개 모듈로 구성되며, 해당 4 개 모듈분 부품·소재 공급

### Page 5

두산에너빌리티

Eugene Research Center _5 
Q23. 중동 지정학적 불안정에 따른 프로젝트·물류 영향 
- 기존 프로젝트의 운송비 증가분을 발주처·개발사와 협의 중 
- 신규 계약에는 비용 보상 및 공기 지연 관련 조항 반영

Q24. 가스터빈 생산능력 증설 규모 및 시기 
- 현재 생산능력 증설 중이며 2031년 출하분까지 예약 접수 
- 시장 수요에 따라 추가 증설 검토

Q25. 2030년 가스터빈 예약 물량의 실제 계약 전환 시점 
- 2030년 출하 물량은 대부분 내년 실제 계약 여부 결정 전망 
- 2031년 출하 물량은 2028년경 계약 여부 결정 예상

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에 동 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

투자기간 및 투자등급/투자의견 비율 
종목추천 및 업종추천 투자기간: 12개월  (추천기준일 종가대비 추천종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함) 당사 투자의견 비율(%)

ㆍBUY(매수) 추천기준일 종가대비 +15%이상 ~ +50%미만 94% 
ㆍHOLD(중립) 추천기준일 종가대비 -10%이상 ∼ +15%미만 3%

Document 324: Apollo拟豪掷750亿美元采购AI芯片，规模远超任何一家Neocloud

Source PDF: all report pdf/Apollo拟豪掷750亿美元采购AI芯片，规模远超任何一家Neocloud.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-07
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

私募巨头 Apollo 正以 750 亿美元的惊⼈押注重塑 AI 芯⽚融资格局 ——350 亿美元购买⾕歌
TPU 租赁给 Anthropic ， 400 亿美元购⼊英伟达 Vera Rubin 芯⽚租赁给 SpaceX 。这⼀规模
远超最⼤ neocloud CoreWeave 的 270 亿美元，使 Apollo 跻⾝仅次于亚⻢逊、微软、⾕歌的
全球芯⽚储备第四极。
摘要：
私募巨头 Apollo Global Management 正以前所未有的规模押注 AI 芯⽚市场，其两笔待完成的芯
⽚交易合计⾦额⾼达 750 亿美元，不仅远超最⼤ neocloud CoreWeave ⾃ 2023 年以来累计 270 亿
美元的芯⽚融资规模，更是排名第⼆的竞争对⼿的逾 10 倍。
据 The Information 最新报道，SpaceX 宣布将租⽤价值 400 亿美元的英伟达 Vera Rubin 芯⽚，⽽
Apollo 将通过旗下⼦公司购⼊这批芯⽚后再租赁给 SpaceX 。此前， Apollo 已于今年 6 ⽉宣布联

合 Broadcom 与 Blackstone ，为其购买⾕歌张量处理器（ TPU ）并租赁给 Anthropic 的交易安排了
350 亿美元融资。
⼀旦上述交易完成，在 AI 芯⽚持有规模上，Apollo 将仅次于亚⻢逊、微软、⾕歌等超⼤规模云
服务商，成为全球芯⽚储备最雄厚的⾮超⼤规模玩家，彻底改变 AI 基础设施的竞争格局。 Apollo
在这些交易中通过撮合交易及管理融资基⾦赚取费⽤。
两笔巨型交易奠定芯⽚霸主地位
Apollo 当前的芯⽚布局由两笔核⼼交易构成。
其⼀是今年 6 ⽉宣布的 350 亿美元交易：Apollo 联合 Broadcom 与 Blackstone ，购买⾕歌 TPU 并租
赁给 AI 初创公司 Anthropic 。其⼆是上周随 SpaceX 公告浮出⽔⾯的 400 亿美元交易：Apollo 通过
⼦公司购⼊英伟达 Vera Rubin 芯⽚，再租赁给⻢斯克旗下的 SpaceX 。
两笔交易合计 750 亿美元，使 Apollo 在芯⽚融资领域的体量远超任何⼀家 neocloud 。据 The

Information 汇编的数据， CoreWeave ⾃ 2023 年以来通过芯⽚抵押融资累计筹资 270 亿美元，位
居 neocloud 之⾸；其后依次为 Valor （ 54 亿美元）、 Nscale （ 49 亿美元）、 IREN （ 39 亿美元）、
Lambda （ 15 亿美元）、 Crusoe （ 11 亿美元）、 Nebius （ 7.75 亿美元）及 Sharon AI （ 3.56 亿美
元）。
值得注意的是，上述统计仅涵盖以 GPU 和 TPU 为抵押的融资，不包括各家 neocloud 为运营融资
⽽发⾏的可转换债券及信贷额度。此外，上周另有⼀笔由花旗集团、美国银⾏与 Blackstone 联合
安排、规模达 630 亿美元的交易 —— ⽤于为 Anthropic 采购 Broadcom 芯⽚提供融资 —— 亦未纳⼊
上述⽐较，原因是各⽅未披露这批芯⽚的实际持有⽅。
芯⽚融资模式： SPV 结构的规模化复制
Apollo 所采⽤的芯⽚融资结构，脱胎于 CoreWeave 与 Blackstone 在 2023 年共同开创的特殊⽬的

载体（ SPV ）模式。在这⼀安排下， neocloud 与其⾦融合作⽅共同设⽴ SPV ，由 SPV 发⾏债务
以购买芯⽚，芯⽚终端⽤⼾⽀付的租⾦则⽤于偿还债务。
Apollo 的操作逻辑与此⼀脉相承，但规模更为庞⼤。与许多 neocloud 不同的是， Apollo 并未额外
投⼊资本建设⽤于存放芯⽚的数据中⼼，其商业模式更为聚焦 —— 通过购买芯⽚、出租给终端
⽤⼾，赚取交易撮合费⽤及基⾦管理费。

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

超⼤规模云服务商则⽆需借助 SPV 结构，可直接通过⾃由现⾦流、股权发⾏或传统公司债完成
芯⽚采购，这也是 Apollo 与超⼤规模云服务商之间的本质区别。
保险资本驱动 Apollo 转型
Apollo 的芯⽚豪赌，折射出这家⽼牌私募机构过去⼗年的深刻转型。 Apollo 由亿万富翁 Leon
Black 联合创⽴，成⽴已逾 36 年，早年以杠杆收购闻名，其中包括 2008 年斥资近 300 亿美元收购
赌场巨头哈拉斯娱乐。
过去⼗年间， Apollo ⼤规模吸纳来⾃保险公司的资本。保险资⾦偏好收益率⾼于投资级债券、
但信⽤评级仍维持投资级的资产类别，⽽芯⽚融资交易恰好契合这⼀需求。这⼀资本结构的转
变，为 Apollo 在 AI 基础设施领域的⼤规模布局提供了充裕的弹药。

芯⽚融资对 Apollo ⽽⾔是⼀个全新的业务⽅向，但其背后的逻辑与传统私募信贷如出⼀辙：以资
产为抵押，提供结构化融资，赚取稳定的利差收益。随着 AI 算⼒需求持续膨胀， Apollo 正将这⼀
逻辑推向新的量级。

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
As expected, the RBA Monetary Policy Board (MPB) lifted
the cash rate 25bps to 4.85% at its November meeting.
This follows the increase at the previous (late-September)
meeting and is a marked turnaround from the outlook back
in August. The post-meeting media statement cited higher

global energy prices resulting from the Middle East conflict,
tighter domestic capacity pressures and an increase in
inflation expectations for the decision.
However, the decision was a split vote, 6 votes to 3, a
possibility we have flagged for some time. In the post-meeting
media conference, Governor Bullock explained that the
dissenting minority put more weight on the downside risks and
did not see signs of wages growth picking up as much as the

staff forecasts implied. Those members were reportedly more
confident that slower growth would prevent higher inflation
expectations becoming entrenched.
Financial conditions are restrictive and this is expected to
bring inflation down over time. Because the RBA assesses the
risks to the inflation outlook to still be skewed to the upside,
the outlook for coming meetings will still be for a decision
between holding and hiking. But we note that the post-meeting

statement omitted September’s language “including increasing
the cash rate target further if needed” from the commitment to
do what is necessary to get inflation down.
In the post-meeting media conference, the Governor
commented that after five hikes, this is probably a good time
to “take stock”. Together with the dissents, we read this as
confirming our current view that the MPB will keep rates
on hold in December. There is also considerable data flow

between now and then, which could go either way, including
the Q3 national accounts.
Similarly, a lot could happen between now and February, but
we see it as the next most likely timing for a hike, should one
eventuate. We still see that as a risk rather than a base case.
Revised forecasts published in the accompanying Statement
on Monetary Policy (SMP) show underlying inflation lingering
around current rates for longer. This contrasts with the
previous SMP forecasts in August, which showed trimmed

mean inflation returning to the 2–3%yr target range by late
2027 and a little below the 2.5%yr target midpoint in 2028.
We note, however, that the conditioning assumptions on oil
prices, which are based on futures prices, were at a local peak
at the time the forecasts were finalised.
RBA hikes cash rate to new high of 4.85%, a December pause likely
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
The RBA’s GDP growth forecasts for 2027 and 2028 were
both revised down –0.3%pts to 1.3% for 2027 and 1.5% in

2028, despite an upward revision to the near term. This is
well below even the RBA’s downbeat view of potential output
growth. Within the GDP aggregate, recent misses and leading
indicators have required upward revisions to the near-term
outlooks for business and dwelling investment, and downside
revisions to public demand. These revisions were offset further
out with downward revisions to the 2028 outlook for business
and dwelling investment and an upward revision to public

demand. Household consumption forecasts were revised down
across most of the profile.
With the unemployment rate already at 4.7% in August and
September – a rate the RBA did not forecast until end-2027 in
its August SMP forecasts – the RBA has lifted its unemployment
forecast to show a peak of 5.1% in at end-2028, the end of the
forecast horizon. This largely reflects stronger labour supply,
with forecasts for employment growth essentially unchanged.

We have repeatedly flagged that cost-of-living pressures are
likely to keep people in the labour force and thus boost the
participation rate. The RBA had previously not factored this
adequately into its forecasts but acknowledged in the SMP
that this mechanism had kept participation higher than it had
expected at the time of the August SMP.
However, because inflation surprised to the upside recently,
this additional labour supply has had no bearing on the

RBA’s assessment of capacity pressures. The RBA assesses
the domestic economy to still be a little tight, and tighter
than previously judged. The RBA’s framework for assessing
the degree of spare capacity or tightness in the economy
necessarily translates an upside surprise on inflation (or unit
labour costs) into a conclusion that the economy must be
tighter than previously judged. Indeed, the RBA’s estimate
of the NAIRU (unemployment rate consistent with inflation

stable at target) has been revised up to 4.75%.
The RBA highlighted that elevated inflation expectations may
be contributing to higher realised inflation. Consumer inflation
expectations have started to lift again but remain below where
they peaked earlier this year. However, the RBA appears
“RBA lifts cash rate 25bps to 4.85% in split
vote, citing “broadly based” domestic inflation
pressures, Middle East conflict and AI boom."

to be putting more attention on the inflation expectations
of firms and the frequency of mentions of pricing in their
liaison program. This is putting a lot of weight on proprietary
information that is not clearly validated by other, longer-
running data sources.

The AI boom is heavily cited in the SMP and other post-
meeting communication. The RBA has lifted its forecasts
for major trading partners, especially high-income Asian
economies that are the centre of high-tech manufacturing.
Demand for AI-related goods is also cited as a driver of both
global and domestic inflation. And although the RBA has
revised up its near-term forecasts for business investment
on the back of strong data centre activity, it is working on

the assumption that Australia does not become ‘a significant
regional hub for data centres’. Nor does it allow for any boost
to productivity trends from the technology, even in 2028. This
is a very different view of the risks from AI than the RBNZ
expressed last week. For the RBA, it is all about inflation, a
little bit about pressure on domestic construction resources,
and nothing about either an ugly unwind of the current
financing boom or a future productivity dividend.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: whiplash
total to $11.1bn. This is another clear marker of the scale of
Australia’s data-centre investment boom, which should feature
prominently in both business investment and imports in the Q3
National Accounts.
Offshore, the world has been focused on the US mid-term

elections. In the House, Senate and a series of State Governor
races, the Democrats experienced resounding victory. Come
2027, the Democrats will have a comfortable majority in both
houses of Congress and are expected to make use of this
strength to respond to voter concerns, particularly over cost-
of-living pressures. The election result also limits President
Trump’s ability to fund an extended military action against
Iran. Notably, both immediately before and after the election,

President Trump and the administration have exhibited a greater
interest in furthering negotiations with Iran. It is therefore not
a surprise that Brent oil has backed off its most recent highs
around USD118 last Friday, although the scale of the drop to
near USD93 yesterday is arguably too optimistic. Brent has since
traded back up to above USD99, still 16% below Friday’s peak.
While the initial reaction to the US election result was a
robust broad-based rally in major equity indexes, concerns

over the potential for tighter regulation of AI and the related
infrastructure buildout jolted US equities lower overnight – the
Nasdaq falling 5.5% and S&P500 4.4%, both a multiple of
the prior session’s gains. That said, US term interest rates and
FOMC rate expectations have also been scaled back; so, while
tech may come under greater scrutiny, a foundation for better
outcomes across the rest of the US economy may become
evident in time. US data out this week continued to suggest

their economy is in robust health overall. And, if tensions in
the Middle East abate, the FOMC’s concerns over US inflation
should subside. Gaining enough comfort for the FOMC to not
hike in December is a high bar, but the probability of further
hikes in 2027 should continue to fall.
Turning then to the UK, the Bank of England raised Bank Rate
by 25bps to 4.00% at their November meeting as expected.
On the Bank's baseline expectation, UK inflation is forecast to

rise from 3.5%yr at September to 5.00% by mid-2027. Activity
data has also surprised to the upside in recent months, though
"there remains limited evidence of knock-on effects on other
prices and wages".
In Australia, the RBA delivered another 25bp rate increase
at its November meeting, taking the cash rate to 4.85%. The
move was widely expected, reflecting the Board’s continued
concern around inflation following the recent run higher in
global energy prices, firmer domestic capacity pressures and

signs that inflation expectations have edged up. Inflation
is now not expected to return to the mid-point of the
target band until mid-2028. This is despite weaker growth
expectations and the unemployment rate trending up to
5.1% by end-2028 on the RBA’s forecasts.
Importantly, though, the decision was not unanimous, with
three members preferring to remain on hold given downside
risks to activity and the restraining influence of slower growth.
Governor Bullock’s suggestion that it was time to “take stock”

after five rate increases also struck a less hawkish note,
while the statement dropped September’s explicit reference
to raising rates further “if needed”. In our view, February
represents the next plausible opportunity for another hike, but
we see this only as a risk.
Policy’s effect on housing is becoming increasingly visible.
Cotality home prices fell a further 1.2% in October to now
be 6.7% below March’s peak. The cumulative price decline
is approaching the scale of previous housing corrections but

appears to have regained momentum after September’s rate
increase. Dwelling approvals  saw a 5.1% headline gain in
September, but this was driven by volatile unit approvals while
detached house approvals fell sharply, suggesting higher rates
and the established-market downturn are beginning to weigh
on the construction pipeline.
Official data on nominal household spending  meanwhile
rose 0.6% in September and 2.1% for Q3; however, much of
the recent acceleration looks to be price-driven, particularly

for fuel. Real spending volumes expanded by a much more
modest (but still solid) 0.7% in Q3.
The Westpac-DataX Consumer Panel provides a granular look at
income, spending and saving flows. The latest update suggests
that, beneath the resilient headline story, households are
increasingly being squeezed, particularly those with low savings
buffers. With another rate increase now delivered, housing
wealth declining and sentiment weak, Q3 increasingly looks like

the high-water mark for growth in consumer spending.
Lastly for Australia, in September the goods trade surplus
widened sharply to $2.8bn, with non-monetary gold trade
yet again adding considerable noise month-to-month. The
most notable development was another exceptionally strong
result for data-processing equipment imports, taking the Q3
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
In the press conference, Governor Bailey recognised the
nascent improvement in energy price pressures over the
past 48 hours and the effectiveness of monetary policy but
also conveyed that "Unless progress towards resolving the
conflict is made, or there is other news which softens the risk

to inflationary pressures, a further increase in Bank Rate may
be warranted". Governor Bailey's tone and comments made in
the minutes by other Committee members did not suggest this
was a decision that needs to be made in December, but rather
a risk to be judged as additional information is received. This
points to further tightening being more likely in early 2027
than before, and only if conditions / risks warrant.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
No deals are done
This week’s developments underscore our message that
a rate hike at the RBNZ’s December meeting is far from a
done deal. New Zealand’s labour market remains softer
than expected, and that will be noted by those on the

policy committee who question the extent of second-round
inflation pressures. The rout in the US mid-term elections
has also taken significant heat out of oil prices for now,
although it’s unclear whether that situation will hold through
to next month. Our view on the endpoint for this OCR cycle
remains unchanged, but there’s a growing risk that the RBNZ
could slow the pace of tightening in the new year.
The September quarter labour market surveys pointed to

ongoing softness in the jobs market. The unemployment
rate rose from 5.6% to 5.7%, continuing a four-year run
of increases, and reaching its highest level since 2015.
Employment was softer than expected, and wage growth
remains subdued despite increasing cost-of-living pressures.
The Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS) showed a surprise
0.1% fall in employment in the September quarter, after a
downwardly revised 0.3% increase in the June quarter. That

went against the Monthly Employment Indicator (MEI) measure
of filled jobs, which had suggested some upward momentum
emerging in the jobs market (and notably, the HLFS measure
had outpaced the MEI for the previous nine straight quarters).
The fall in HLFS employment was accompanied by a fall in
the labour force participation rate from 70.6% to 70.4%.
These co-movements essentially reversed the surprisingly
large increases that we saw in the June quarter, which we had

suggested at the time were more likely due to sampling error
in the survey rather than a genuine surge.
We generally recommend focusing on the unemployment rate,
which is less vulnerable to sampling errors – and the message
here is one of softness. Our best interpretation is that, to the
extent that jobs are growing, it’s not fast enough to absorb the
growth in the population (which itself is a fairly low bar, as net
migration remains at a low ebb).

Even with the economy returning to growth over the last
year or so, hiring activity has remained weak. This may be
an ongoing legacy of employment trends in previous years.
Businesses have told us that they felt burned by the severe
skill shortages that developed over 2021-23, and looked to
hold on to workers to the extent they could during the 2024-
25 downturn. The flipside of this is that they may now find
themselves overstaffed even as activity picks up again.

This shows up most strikingly in labour force transition rates.
The rise in unemployment over the last few years has not
been driven by an increase in job losses, but by accumulation
of people who, once out of work, are finding it increasingly
difficult to get back in again. That also extends to young
people trying to enter the workforce for the first time – youth
unemployment of around 25% is the highest in the history of
the survey going back to 1986.

The Labour Cost Index (LCI) rose by 0.5% in the September
quarter, in line with our expectations overall but a bit weaker
in the details – private sector wages were up by 0.5%, while
there was a surprisingly large 0.7% increase in public sector
wages. There was a notable jump in the number of employers
citing cost-of-living adjustments as a reason for increasing pay
rates – although the size of those increases varied a great deal,
with many substantially less than the headline inflation rate.

Potential RBNZ implications
The labour market data was unequivocally weaker than
expected by either us or the RBNZ. While likely not a
gamechanger for the cycle overall, it will add ammunition to the
arguments of the doves on the Monetary Policy Committee that
a pause in interest rate rises will make sense soon – perhaps in
December or early next year. It’s likely the case that interest rate
markets were over-pricing the odds of a hike at the 9 December

MPS, so the subsequent pullback in pricing makes sense.
The message from the RBNZ in the October meeting was to
be careful about pricing in "done deals", and that the outlook
Michael Gordon
Senior Economist

### Page 7

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
7
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
will be data-dependent. As we noted at the time, alongside
measures of inflation expectations, the key domestic data
to watch ahead of the RBNZ’s 9 December meeting will
be indicators of what is happening to excess capacity (the
output gap), as this will be a key determinant of the Bank’s

assessment of the outlook for inflation over the coming year.
The RBNZ looks at a broad suite of indicators of labour market
slack, although their research found that only four of them
seem to have predictive power: the unemployment rate,
the vacancy to unemployment ratio, the QSBO measure of
labour as the main limiting factor to growth, and the rate of
transition between jobs. The first three of these all pointed
to a softening in the September quarter. (The fourth one is

calculated from income tax data, so it’s not publicly available
and even the RBNZ receives it only with a lag.)
In the eyes of the dovish MPC members, the softness in
the labour market indicators will likely push in the direction
of having greater comfort with the medium-term inflation
outlook. The outcome of the election, with the possibility of a
return to a dual inflation/employment mandate, could increase
the prominence of these factors. This likely won’t be an explicit

factor in the RBNZ’s reasoning at its December review, but it
may be in the minds of individual MPC members.
That said, it’s important to remember that in the RBNZ’s
forecasting framework, the GDP figures are the main
determinant of excess capacity in the economy. The most
recent GDP data tells us that the output gap probably
narrowed a bit through the June quarter, mainly reflecting
historical revisions. In light of the latest labour market figures,

there may be some downside risk to the outlook for Q3 GDP –
but there are conflicting indicators there, so it’s not that clear.
At last month’s surprise on-hold OCR decision, Governor
Breman lamented that there was no protocol for the RBNZ
to be able to signal how it was reading the economic data –
in particular the softer than expected Q3 CPI print – ahead
of the policy meeting. In that light, it will be interesting to
see whether they use some already-scheduled speaking

engagements to signal what they make of the labour market
data. Top of mind is Chief Economist Conway's 10 November
speech to the UBS Australasia Conference (pointedly titled
“How is the NZ economy tracking”). Governor Breman is also
speaking to the Institute of Directors on 19 November.
Our view remains that the December OCR hike is more likely
than not. But there will be a clear debate to be had between
the MPC hawks and doves, and it may again come down to a

vote. We still expect a 4% peak in the OCR for this cycle, but
the time taken to get there could be more flexible. Takes along
the lines of "3% is the terminal rate" seem rather hasty on the
basis of a single data release.
Aside from how the Committee interprets the labour market
data, the key considerations in December will be: the RBNZ’s
surveys of inflation expectations by households and businesses;
whether the US mid-term elections lead to a sustained de-

escalation in the Middle East and a pullback in oil prices; and
whether developments in the AI space exacerbate some MPC
members’ concerns about the global economic environment.
And then of course there’s the result of the General Election,
with voting closing on Saturday evening. The actual formation
of a government is likely to take weeks of negotiation, so the
outcome may not be known by the 9 December MPS but at least
the range of possibilities should be clearer. We’ll publish our

initial thoughts on the election results early on Monday morning.

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.8
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
NZ: Oct Retail Card Spending (%mth)
Nov 12, Last: –0.4, Westpac f/c: 1.4
We’re forecasting a 1.4% rise in retail spending in October.
While that would be a solid increase, it’s in large part due to
the 11% rise in petrol prices over the past month. Stripping out

fuel spending, we’re forecasting a 0.6% rise in core spending
categories. Core spending has been very choppy recently,
and the increase we’re assuming follows falls in the past few
months. A result in line with our expectations would leave the
trend in core spending relatively flat since the start of this year
with the rise in fuel prices weighing on spending in other areas.
AUS: Nov Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment (index)
Nov 10, Last: 84.4
Consumer sentiment fell 4.7% to 80.4 in Oct, returning to the

deeply pessimistic levels seen earlier in the year. Responses
over the course of the survey week showed a very sharp
deterioration after the RBA rate hike.
The Nov survey is in the field over the week ended Nov 7.
Interest rates will again factor with the RBA raising the cash
rate by another 25bps to 4.85%, an 18yr high. Average pump
prices also pushed higher, the national average just under
$2.40/litre, while the housing market downturn has continued

to broaden and deepen. The US mid-term election results may
have some effects on sentiment locally as well.
AUS: Q3 Housing Finance Approvals (%qtr)
Nov 11 Last: –5.2, Westpac f/c: –10
Market f/c: –8.5, Range: –15 to –5
The total value of housing finance approvals fell The value of
new dwelling finance approvals excl. refinance fell 5.2%qtr,
the largest quarterly drop in three years. Annual growth eased
to 7.3%yr. The weakness was accounted for mainly by lower

lending volumes: loan numbers were down 4.0%qtr (–0.6%yr),
while average loan size also contributed negatively.
Q3 is expected to see an even bigger decline with the full
impact of interest rate rises and tax policy changes showing
through. The value of both loan enquiries and turnover fell by
around 10% in the quarter. We expect total finance approvals
to show a 10% drop as well, with investor loan approvals likely
to be down 16%qtr and owner occupier approvals down 6%qtr.

### Page 9

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
9
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
US: Oct CPI (%mth)
Nov 11, Headline, Last: 0.6, Market f/c: 0.3, Westpac f/c: 0.4
US consumer prices rose by 0.6% in September, although
half of the gain was due to energy inflation, with core prices
instead up 0.3%. Over the year, prices were reported to have

risen 3.7%yr overall and 2.6%yr excluding food and energy.
Constructively, ex food, energy and shelter, annual inflation
met the FOMC’s medium-term target of 2.0%yr.
Come October, energy price pressures are set to recede
given the pre-election run higher in oil prices came late in the
month. A softer print for shelter is also likely to moderate core
momentum. We see a 0.4% gain for the headline measure and
a 0.2% result for core inflation as most probable. Looking to

2027, disinflation is expected to continue at a measured pace
towards the medium-term target.

Editorial 
Increasing signs of a bottom 
Strategy summary: Democrats’ thumping win in the US mid -
term elections has three key implications: i) policy gridlock is 
back in Washington, likely stalling fiscal expansion and new 
tariffs; ii) the chance of a prolonged Middle East conflict is 
curtailed, lowering oil prices and inflation expectations; and  iii) 
the pace of AI data-centre buildout is at risk , curbing AI-linked 
bond supply. Higher corporate and wealth taxes are a long-term 
risk if Democrats win the 2028 presidential elections.   
For portfolios, the election reinforce s our mid-cycle playbook 
already in place:  remaining broadly balanced across assets, 
while looking for tactical opportunities. As the S&P500 volatility 
index (VIX) surpasses 25, we see rising signs of equity market 
bottoming. We add an opportunistic idea on the US IT sector 
(see page 4), while using elevated volatility to earn income 
through structured strategies. In bonds, we prefer the 3-7-year 
part of the Developed Market bond yield curve and BBB-to-BB-
rated corporate bonds to earn attractive income as AI supply 
eases. In FX, we position for a modestly weaker USD over 6 -
12 months by allocating to ex-US assets.  
Policy implications:  Oil fell 12% over the past week as 
Democrats captured the US Senate and House. The party also 
flipped governorships in five states – Georgia, Iowa, Nevada, 
Ohio and Texas – securing a majority of governorships among 
50 US states for the first time since 2010. Vice President Vance 
and Republican leaders attributed the defeat to the Iran conflict, 
making it harder for President Trump to sustain it. Democrat 
control of the defence budget from next year further constrains 
conflict escalation. Despite near-term escalation risks, we see 
the geopolitical risk premium in oil prices fading, with oil easing 
towards USD 80/bbl  over 6-12 months. Oil-driven disinflation 
should allow central banks to adopt less aggressive policies. 
Adding tactical exposure to US IT sector : Semiconductors 
slumped this week amid concerns over data-centre moratoria, 
while beneficiaries of Democrat policies include defensives, 
affordability-related stocks, tariff rollback beneficiaries and  the 
healthcare sector. We use the volatility to add tactical exposure 
to the US IT sector . As we enter mid -cycle dynamics, equities 
should return 8-10% annually. The 2015–2017 mid-cycle offers 
a useful comparison: with an opposition Congress,  S&P 500 
returns slowed to 8.3% , but remained positive. Diversification 
across sectors and regions remains a priority amid the rotation. 
Prefer belly of the bond curve: Government bonds rallied on 
expectations of slower growth. We still expect two more Fed 
hikes by H1 2027, given sticky near-term inflation and a robust 
economy. However, cheaper oil should lower inflation towards 
the Fed’s 2% target next year, allowing rate cuts in H2 2027. 
Ultra-long yields remain elevated due to  fiscal challenges. 
Bonds, particularly those from non-hyperscaler investment -
grade corporates , offer attractive income. The 3 -7-year 
segment – the belly of the yield curve – offers the best balance 
of income and sensitivity to interest-rate changes. 
Credit to benefit from restrained AI supply:  Since states  
control data centre regulations, Democrat wins in governorship 
races in leading data centre states, such as Texas and Georgia, 
means growth in this segment is likely to be restricted. Slower 
data-centre construction could improve credit supply -demand 
conditions due to reduced issuance by hyperscalers. However, 
some data -centre-focused deals could become less viable, 
warranting caution towards lower -quality issuers. High -yield 
credit spreads have widened, but BB- and B-rated bonds have 
held up, while CCC -rated debt has r eached distressed levels. 
We prefer the BBB–BB range, spanning the boundary between 
investment-grade and high-yield debt, for income. 
A modest headwind for the USD, tailwind for gold: The 
dollar should remain range-bound near term before weakening 
modestly over 6-12 months. Democrat control of the Congress 
limits tariff escalation , fiscal expansion and rapid AI buildout , 
three pillars of US exceptionalism supporting the USD through 
2026. Disinflation should also encourage a less aggressive Fed 
stance, removing another source of USD support. Near -term 
support for the USD index (DXY) remains at 100.7 . A weaker-
than-expected payrolls report tonight could accelerate the 
USD’s decline, and vice versa . A softer dollar and sustained 
central-bank buying, led by China, remain supportive of gold.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

### Page 4

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 6 November 2026

Top client questions 
  What are the equity market implications of the US mid -
term election results?   
Our view: The US mid -term election outcome adds political 
headwinds but does not alter our outlook for positive equity 
returns. Investors should maintain a Core allocation to global 
equities and continue to diversify their equity exposure across 
regions and sectors. We initiate an opportunistic idea on the US 
information technology (IT) sector, moving our opportunistic 
idea positioning from a de fensive tilt to a more balanced 
positioning. Higher volatility also presents opportunities to use 
structured strategies for equity exposure.  
Rationale: The Democrats’ victory in both chambers of Congress 
has supported the outperformance of themes aligned with their 
policy priorities , with clean energy stocks , including solar 
companies, among the notable beneficiaries. Affordability-related 
themes have also benefited, including companies that could gain 
from potential tariff rollbacks. Medicaid-related stocks  have also 
risen on hopes that healthcare will gain from policy changes. 
Meanwhile, semiconductor stocks remain under pressure  amid 
concerns about potential moratoriums on data centres. Our AI capital 
expenditure (capex) estimates remain conservative relative to 
consensus, but with a potential reset in expectations around data 
centre spending, investors should remain selective.  
We initiate an opportunistic idea on the US IT sector after a 
significant 17% correction this quarter. Historically, this has been 
followed by strong positive returns over the next few quarters ( see 
table). Semiconductors, about 40% of US IT, appears oversold after 
correcting more than 30% since we closed our opportunistic idea a 
month ago. Earnings growth for US IT continues to be solid, with 
volume driven growth in semiconductors, AI monetisation 
opportunities, ongoing cloud growth and resilient services revenue.  
As we highlighted in our November 2026 Global Market Outlook , 
the transition to mid -cycle is typically characterised by greater 
political headwinds. The mid -term election outcome reinforces this 
trend. The mid-cycle experience of 2015-17 offers a useful historical 
parallel for returns that moderate, but remain positive. Although we 
trimmed global equities to a Core allocation last week, we continue 
to expect equities to deliver mid-to-high single-digit returns per 
annum during the mid -cycle, even amid political headwinds, 
supported by steady earnings growth in both 2027 and 2028. 
Stay invested and diversify, with Overweight allocations to the US 
and A xJ, which offer the strongest earnings growth. Sector 
diversification is key as we expect market leadership to broaden. We 
are Overweight the US IT, US healthcare, US financial, US materials, 
Europe ex-UK financial and Japan financial sectors. 
—  Fook Hien Yap, Senior Investment Strategist

Policy uncertainty and a change in the innovation 
cycle is consistent with our view of a mid-cycle 
transition, where equity returns moderate but 
remain positive

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 6 November 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What are the key takeaways from Q3 2026 US earnings? 
Our view:  Q3 US earnings remain robust, but more modest 
earnings beats reinforce our view that equity markets are 
entering a mid-cycle phase. We remain Overweight US equities, 
but recommend investors to maintain diversified sector 
exposure as returns moderate and market leadership broadens. 
Rationale: As of 30 October, 82% of S&P500 companies that ha d 
reported had beaten earnings estimates. However, aggregate 
earnings were just 4.5% above expectations, a smaller beat than in 
recent quarters. Still, Q3 US earnings growth remain s robust at 
31.6% y/y, supporting expected full-year growth of 36.1% in 2026 
and 16.4% in 2027 for the S&P500. The slower pace of upgrades 
suggests the bull market is maturing  as investors focus on the 
sustainability of earnings growth. This raises the hurdle for positive 
share price reactions and makes selectivity more essential. For Big 
Tech, capex commitments remain high, while free cash flow is under 
pressure and expected to remain negative. Financing risks weigh on 
sentiment, particularly for companies with weaker balance sheets.  
We remain Overweight US equities , given the strong earnings 
growth, but we expect elevated volatility  and modest returns. We 
prefer quality exposure within technology  while maintaining  
diversified exposure across US financials, healthcare and materials. 
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist 
  What to expect from Japanese banks’ upcoming results?   
Our view:  We expect  Japanese banks’  earnings growth  to 
continue over the medium term, supported by higher domestic 
net interest income (NII) and resilient fee income. We remain 
positive on the sector, favouring banks with strong deposit 
franchises and scope to increase shareholder returns. 
Investors will focus on whether full -year guidance upgrades 
and buybacks exceed expectations. 
Rationale: The BoJ’s September rate hike to 1.25% took effect late 
in the quarter, so its benefit should be more visible from the 
December quarter as loans reprice. We expect three further BoJ 
rate hikes by June 2027, supporting further NII expansion.  We 
will also look for signs that stronger corporate investment is driving 
faster loan growth and higher financing and advisory fees.  
We prefer to place more weight on guidance upgrades driven by 
recurring lending and fee income than on those driven by gains on 
equity disposals. Continued reductions in cross -shareholdings 
could free up capital for additional buybacks, providing a further 
boost to return on equity  (ROE). Key risks:  faster-than-expected 
deposit rate repricing, rising credit costs and bond portfolio losses. 
—  Robin Xie, CFA, Senior Investment Strategist
